ParlaMint-AT_2006-09-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00163_d8e2784	Mr President. Mrs Frauenminister! Ladies and gentlemen! We have this current hour, the last one before the 1. October, before the election, dedicated to a human group in Austria, which in the last seven years has gone no better, but worse, namely the Austrian women. Our aim is to ensure that this group of people – 52 percent of the Austrian population – can still imagine whether – first – the problems are taken seriously by the political parties and, second, what solutions they can put on the table. The biggest problem still remains the income shears. I brought this with me today to make it clear. [[the speaker shows a graphic with the caption “Gender Pay Gap”]] This is the ‘woman euro’; one third is missing. And this has even worsened dramatically in recent years. If you look at a woman's working life, it becomes clear that a man in Austria earns more than one house on average, high to 40 years of employment, €250 000. So only the fact that you are a woman in Austria is a disadvantage in the labour market – and at the end of a working life as a woman you have to find that you have earned a total of €250 000 less. The Black and Blue Federal Government has done nothing to change this over the past seven years. On the contrary: The figures we now find on the labour market are depressing, disillusioning and very sad! Meanwhile, almost every second woman works only more part-time. If it was 20 to 23 percent ten years ago, it is now more than 40 percent of women who work part-time in Austria. This means half the salary or part of the salary, which means worse chances of rise, which means worse career opportunities, and that means, above all, poverty, especially poverty in old age. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] The Austrian Federal Government and its representatives are now always trying to talk about this by talking about increasing women's employment. I'd like to take this right away, so no one's confused: It is spoken of as “increasing women’s employment”. But that's wrong! If you divide the entire working time, full working time, you will realize it is available for women in Austria after these seven years less work than it was seven years ago. They argue exclusively with additional part-time jobs that prove to bring women into precarious employment situations that do not make their lives easier for them but make them more difficult. In the meantime, there are many women in part-time jobs who do not want to, but have to, and this is a problem that you completely ignore. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] Every day there are 18 more women who have made this government unemployed, 18 more women every day! Even if you hide some of them in training, that doesn't change the terrifying number. We have over 120,000 unemployed women, and above all we have many women who are at home, 70 000 women who want to work but cannot do so because of the framework conditions. This is particularly sad, because in all other countries this is going in a positive direction. In the Scandinavian countries as well as in the EU average, as well as in our neighbouring countries, female employment is increasing in full-time jobs. Austria is one of the few countries where it does not work, where women's employment is falling. It can therefore be assumed that this is not a natural law or a European law, no European standard, no situation created by external influences, but that it can be influenced and can also be changed by the right policy. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] However, this would require serious acceptance of these problems and not a beautiful speech. I fear that it will come back to you today to speak and ignore this problem. We really want to close the income shears, we want to deal seriously with this problem. This is not quite easy, but there are some very good proposals from us on the table in this regard. We want women to get to the train in top positions – in politics, in the economy, also in administration. It is, I believe, embarrassing that at Austrian universities there is no single rector, but only men, and it is, I believe, also embarrassing, if in public service, so to speak, top-level jobs are still occupied exclusively with men. I would like to give an example here because this federal government is so often bragging about doing so much for women. [[Abg. Amon: A man has also become chairman of the Greens!]] In the Ministry of Life, Environment Minister Pröll, who is as old as I and often describes himself as young and modern, has acted in the post-occupying of two section heads as follows: There were nine section heads, all men. Two of them retired. Many qualified women from the ministry have applied – and two men have been reoccupied! The ratio in the ministry with the top jobs, with the section heads, is still 9 : 0. I think in history, especially in sports history, there have been 9 : 0 withdrawals. I think that's something you can think about in this case. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] Unfortunately, it does not look different here in this house. I think that if you claim to change society and close this big gap of justice, you should start with yourself a little bit. It was a call for let me know what the greens were like. – With the greens, this is very easy: We do not have a body, this also applies to the National Council Group, where the majority of women do not have the majority, and the 50 percent quota is mandatory for us. There is no such thing at all with the ÖVP. There are quotas for ÖAAB and farmers' associations, but obviously not for women, and I find that embarrassing! - No, that's right! [[Congratulation with the greens.]] [[Intervocations at the ÖVP.]] Unfortunately, I cannot ignore the SPÖ either. I think a 40 percent commitment is already a little weak, but I believe that if you can't achieve this, you should stand by it and not speak beautifully. I think it is a matter of course that women in all areas, if you have the opportunity to choose, should receive half-and-half. [[Abg. Steibl: There is freedom of choice in Austria!]] [[Abg. Dipl.-Ing. Scheuch: We have 60 percent female ministers in the government!]] In this context, I find it particularly embarrassing that a German chancellor is proud that in a government among the ministers – not with ministers and State Secretarys together, only with the ministers – half women are women, and that is available with a quote that actually insults the Austrian women. The Federal Chancellor said: "If I were a left-winger, the entire emancentrupt would be flat in front of me." This is an increase of all women in Austria, who are truly on the ground in many situations, which are, for example, in the difficult situation, to have to be single-parents with €470! To be proud of this, and this in such a jargon, Federal Chancellor, is an increase of the Austrian women and a naughtiness towards them! [[Congratulation with the Greens and the SPÖ.]] [[Abg. [[Intervocations at the ÖVP.]] – Do you share this saying? Do you think that's all right? Do you like something like that? Is that the language you communicate with women? – I don’t think we need such a thing in Austria. I think the women should choose you first if you like it. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] It is therefore so sad – and that is why we are making this the subject again today – because we actually have serious problems in Austria. And to do so, it says a lot about how important it is for you to develop Austrian women, their opportunities, their opportunities for the future, their job opportunities. To answer in this way is the sad end point and peak of something that can be described no differently than an increase of women for seven years, not just now, this week. Steibl: Women have freedom of choice!]] – You can speak immediately. I understand you're upset. As a woman, this statement has also affected me. But contact your Chancellor and complain to him! 100 000 additional jobs. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] We would like the AMS to offer more and more qualification measures and thus use money especially for women from 35 onwards who, after the baby “Pause” – “Pause” between quotation marks – do not allow baby leave time to re-enter; these are now four out of ten. This is a very sad balance. And above all, we want different framework conditions in the family policy decision. We want to redefine the child money. We want it to be higher and allow for a shorter period of time and, above all, a more partnership between family work. The current model is the opposite. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] While you have increased the single-earner deposits, you have made a tax reform, but women have had nothing to do with families who want to live in partnership. But that's the future, and if you don't take that into account, then you'll be on 1st. October rightly get presented the bill of people who want to live modernly, who naturally want to choose how to shape family, and don't want it as you think: Freedom of choice is for the woman to stay at home, and commitment to compulsory obligation for the man to stay at home. That's not our answer – on the contrary! [[Congratulation with the greens.]] Same right for both, equal opportunities for both! It is still an economic decision who remains at home. With our karenz model – 80 percent income dependent – there is a very great incentive that – between quotation marks – “the even better Deserving” also has the chance to stay home. It's a wonderful time. I think with 460 € this is not possible for families. Depending on income, this works wonderfully in other countries. Some are exemplary. And it would also be manageable with existing financial resources until the time when each father would go to Karenz for three months. But until then, I think it's a further way. We want to work a little bit at that, and I hope you'll be on 1st. In October and after that, think of a better one and do not forget a whole population group in the long run.

There have also been a number of key measures to promote this positive development, such as an active labour market policy of our Minister for Labour and Economic Affairs or the promotion of the qualification of women. ParlaMint-AT_2006-09-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00163_d8e5104	We now reach 1st. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. The debate opens up to Mrs. Baumgartner-Gabitzer. Your welcome time is 8 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-09-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00163_d8e5131	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen, High house! Agenda item 1 is the report of the Special Committee on the outcome of the negotiations or the report of the Austrian Convention. This is based on a 19-month work of the Austrian Convention – a very hard time, an intensive period of discussion and a very valuable time on the subject of the Constitution. I would like to begin by thanking all the experts who have done a very important job in the Austrian Convention, because Constitutional law is very difficult. I would therefore like to express my express thanks for their participation, even if at the end there is no new constitution. But it is an interesting report at the end. [[Congratulation with the ÖVP and the Abg. Scheibner.]] The main objective of the EIA was to achieve a slim, understandable and also clear constitution in this Convention. Over the decades, our constitution has been phased out, and in the end, its understanding, clarity and clarity have suffered massively. The second essential motivation of the ÖVP was to create a catalogue of fundamental rights and implement it into the Constitution, which also includes fundamental social rights and is based on international standards. The third very essential point was to leave the proven administrative structures on the one hand and, on the other hand, to eliminate double tracks and to improve legal protection on the other. These three objectives were for us, the ÖVP, the most important because they are for the benefit of the citizen. In the course of the discussions, there were many other points on the agenda, whose implementation would have been important for us, such as reorganising and improving the levels of competence, improving the position of the Federal Council and its involvement in the national legislative process and also increasing the legal protection of persons. All these are points whose implementation would have been important. I would now like to draw attention to the most important points, which are those that are oriented towards the citizens. First: Constitutional adjustment. – For us, it was always important to have a constitutional instrument, and in addition, only a few constitutional strobants, these are historical laws, such as the Neutrality Act, the Habsburg Law or the Law on Re-activating. In this respect, the SPÖ and the ÖVP have come very close. Consensus in the next legislative period should therefore be possible. Secondly, Preamble in the Constitution. . We wanted a Preamble in the Constitution, which lays down the main principles of the Constitution. I never understood why this rejected the opposition so vehemently. It is quite common in many, especially the new, modern constitutions of Europe, to have such a part in the Constitution. But I have only experienced a Justament stand and a massive rejection by the other parties in the Convention. The SPÖ has created many new state tasks – state tasks that we do not believe are necessary; and the cost question must also be ignored. We also reject the fact that the state is interfering in countless human affairs, since we consider this not necessary. The third important point for us was the creation of a catalogue of fundamental rights, including social rights. Of course, liberal fundamental rights, classical defence rights to the state, such as the protection of domestic rights or freedom of thought, conscience and religious freedom, are particularly important. These calculations already exist, of course, but our aim is to integrate these rights in a clear form into the constitutional instrument. In addition, we also wanted to have fundamental social rights in the catalogue of fundamental rights, such as the right to reconciliation between family and profession, the right to education and the like. With regard to fundamental social rights, we were particularly important to pay attention to the personal responsibility of fundamental rights, i.e. no state premeditation and no control. In this respect, consensus was not possible. Easily approaching were recognizable. I therefore hope that we will find a solution here too. For decades now, it has been important for us to introduce the letter election. I have never understood, ladies and gentlemen, why there is no consent from the opposition to the introduction of the election of letters. It is a very common development at an international level. Nothing there has been movement on the part of the opposition parties. I regret that. However, we will not tire out constantly demanding the right of choice of letter, because it is a modern person who is very flexible, who knows for himself what he chooses. In my opinion, the fears that this will affect the personal exercise of the right to vote are attracted to the hair. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] The fourth and most important point is administrative structures. We want to remain with the proven ones, like the district captains, we do not want experiments, we do not want to blow up the administration, like the regions proposed by the SPÖ with its own statute, that is absurd in our opinion. The administration must function, especially in the first instance. That is why we also need to maintain the provenness where action is taken quickly. What we need, on the other hand, are the first national administration courts. A Federal Administrative Court, for example on asylum issues, also seems important to us. In this respect, the opposition was approximated. I think it will be possible to find consensus in the next legislative period. Before submitting our motion for a resolution, let me make a brief comment of a general nature. The Convention started at the beginning with incredibly negative accompanying music – accompanying music from sides of the opposition, which has locked itself and has already said from the beginning that such a convention is not necessary. But we believe that this Convention was important. We are glad that this Convention took place, and we believe that it has done good work. I regret that there was no better constructiveness on the part of the opposition parties in the Convention. We believe that we must further develop constitutional law, that we must implement a reform of the administration, and we will not tire of promoting it even in the next legislative period and looking for federal partners here. Now let me come to our motion for a resolution which has already been distributed. I would now like to explain it in its core points or read it to him: [[Quoted print version removed]] My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen of the opposition! I invite you to give this - and I hope you will also believe - very important motion for a resolution. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-09-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00163_d8e6692	Mr President. High house! Ladies and gentlemen! Ladies and gentlemen. The special Committee of the National Council has discussed, inter alia, the following topics in a total of ten meetings: Constitutional adjustment, fundamental rights protection, administrative structure, security policy principles, division of competence, Federal Council and democratic control. Following the deliberations of the Constitutional Convention, the Special Committee tried to find further agreement in the above-mentioned materials or to reveal the differing views. The agreement, adopted at the beginning of the Committee's deliberations, that a contentious decision-making is only possible if all the contentious points of the Constitutional Reform are resolved has facilitated the work to the extent that the Committee was able to agree to deal with the various blocks of the subject under this sign. In this way it was possible to open negotiations in all areas and to find out in which areas there is agreement in principle and also to identify specific parts for a constitutional change which are politically indisputable without having to give final approval. [[President Mag. Prammer shall take over the chairmanship.]] In the Special Committee, it was also necessary to identify those sub-areas which are still controversial between the political groups and in which no agreement is currently possible. It should not be overlooked that there must also be negotiations with other partners outside Parliament on some of the issues that still exist between the political groups in this House. As an example, I refer to the complex and in many ways controversial area of the redeployment of the division of tasks between the federal government and countries. In addition to the deliberations of the political groups represented in the National Council, which represent different models of solution, there will still have to be intensive discussions with the representatives of the federal states. ==References====External links== The work of the Special Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection, to be re-elected in October, is a basis for continuing the debate. It is gratifying that in some areas there has been a fundamental consensus. For example, I call the establishment of national administrative courts, the maintenance of the Neutrality Act and the reform of the Constitution. These foundations can be built so that there is a legitimate hope that, in the next legislative period, an agreement of all political groups in this House could be reached on relevant issues. We need a modern federal constitution for our country; its foundations have been drawn up in the Austrian Convention and in subsequent discussions of the Special Committee. However, I would like to stress that the completion of the work of the Special Committee does not mean the end of the Constitutional Discussion, but rather that an intermediate step has been taken towards a new Austrian federal constitution. Let me expressly formulate a few objectives: a clear constitutional work that is understandable to citizens without creating the numerous individual secondary laws and constitutional provisions. A comprehensive and up-to-date catalogue of fundamental rights should also guarantee fundamental social rights in accordance with the European Charter of Fundamental Rights. The establishment of administrative courts in the countries is intended to ensure a fair, efficient and civil administration as well as an improvement in legal protection. A modern division of tasks linked to the skills of the countries and communities should strengthen the principle of freedom of federalism and the communities as a place of decision-making close to citizens. This requires courage to change, from all sides. After eleven years in the EU, it must finally be understood that it is high time to rethink the division of competence in the light of the integration of Austria into the European context, perhaps to reinvent it in parts, in its core to the 1920s of the last century. In doing so, I do not see federalism as a burden, but as a starting point for a citizen-oriented policy and a service-oriented administration. [[Abg. Ollinger: But without passion presented!]] It is important to find a new balance here, with, of course, the principle that the level that takes over a task should also bear responsibility for it. That this can work well shows the example of the transfer of the Federal Roads area to the countries. [[Abg. Oils: A little more Pathos, please!]] A point that seems important to me in view of the forthcoming national elections is finally a contemporary design of the right to vote. The new Austrian Federal Constitution must also provide timely solutions on this issue. In this sense, I hope for a fruitful continuation of the debate on a new basic government for our country. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-09-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00163_d8e8247	Thank you very much, Madam President. Mr State Secretary! High house! The attempt to recast the Austrian federal constitution was certainly a milestone in the now ending legislative period, with a lot of positive energy being spent. It's just a shame that we didn't get any results. I would now like to address a few specific points, in particular those relating to competence and administration, which would be very important for efficient and efficient management. Firstly, the federalist principle. – I believe that we all agree that this must be maintained, that Austria is a state, and that it is necessary to clearly separate the competences, especially in the legislation between the federal government and countries, and to reorganise them accordingly. Only then will we come to a economical administration. The question of so-called cooperative legislation is still a bit open to me, because it really has to be practical, so that it is clear how federal authorities and countries share responsibility in the legislation. A second point that was discussed was the appreciation of the Federal Council. All parties agree on this too. I am particularly interested in the proposal that should be implemented, namely that the members of the Federal Council can take part in the deliberations of the committees of the National Council and are thus already involved in the current legislative process and not only in retrospect: Here we will put a veto! Another point, the third point that has been discussed, from my point of view, is the question of the principle of legality. We currently have a very casuistic interpretation of the legislation, and the principle of legality is taken so strictly that the laws have to regulate everything up to the smallest detail. It would really be worth considering, which has been put in place, that legislation can also predetermine the behaviour of the administrative authorities in the future by setting targets. I know there are great reservations here, but it certainly seems to be superior for better and simpler legislation. A fourth point, which was always a personal concern to me, is the creation of the national administrative courts. There is agreement here, and it is actually not quite understandable why one has not taken this point out, because there could have been a comprehensive new regulation here without waiting for the whole. It is also important that, in the national administrative courts, it is decided meritorically and not only cassatoryally, so that the applicants can quickly come to a decision. The concerns that there could then be a division and a disunity of the case law must be opposed to the administrative court ensuring the uniformity of the case law in the end. In any case, it is important: The current waiting period for decisions of the Administrative Court is not reasonable. A fifth point. The current administrative structures should be maintained in this way, in particular the first instance, the District Headquarters. The fact that criticism is constantly being practised here and that it is proposed that an elected representation should be set aside for them, or that new levels such as regions should be created, certainly does not make everything easier, but it only makes it more difficult. In this sense you allow me to conclude another personal word: It was a great pleasure and honour for me to belong to the House during this legislative period and to be able to participate in the decision-making of many important laws and matters – I only remember the two enlargements of the European Union. I thank you, ladies and gentlemen, especially for listening to me so often and so attentively, and I wish all those who have listened to the House after the 1. October will again be a member, a lot of success in the work for Austria.

[[Abg. Scheibner: But you want association action!]] If these are not given, then we are only at the level of state objectives, and fundamental rights differ from national objectives which citizens have nothing directly at all. [[Abg. Scheibner: You just want your organization...!]] Of course, the so-called rights of freedom, which guarantee people that they can make their lives free from state violations, are a basis of our constitution. But we must see that almost all European constitutions today have a second element of fundamental rights, and these are fundamental social rights. They are based on the fact that people in modern societies – and this is the important concern – are protected not only from police attacks and arbitrary expropriations, but - with the same self-evidentness, ladies and gentlemen! – also before poverty, homelessness or illness without adequate medical assistance. They therefore include three important areas: a right to work, a right to adequate working conditions and a right to social security. Let me conclude by saying the following! I believe that the social state principle is within the hearts and minds of the Austrians, it is already anchored there. In addition to freedom, social security is at last included in the catalogue of fundamental rights of the Austrian federal constitution, ladies and gentlemen! ParlaMint-AT_2006-09-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00163_d8e9639	We're now on the 2nd. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. We are going into the debate. First of all, Mrs Mag. Lunacek. Desirable time: 7 minutes. Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-09-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00163_d8e9670	Madam President. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! The reason why I am first speaking here is that the Greens will not give this request the consent. This is a proposal for a resolution to include in the Constitution the protection function of the Austrian People's Group in South Tyrol in any constitutional reform and also to anchor the protection function of other states for their groups living in Austria in the Constitution. – I will explain to you why, against all other parties, we cannot agree. The relationship between Austria and South Tyrol and Italy is an excellent one in the last, let's say, 30 years. Many have contributed to this, especially many Tyroleans, but also many other Austrians, that has developed. As early as 1992, there was the dispute resolution; in 2002 – I was already in this House – we also celebrated and found it right here in the House. ==References== September 2006, there was a celebration in South Tyrol – in Bolzano, also in the Bolzano Landtag – on the occasion that 60 years ago the Paris Agreement, the so-called Gruber-De-Gasperi Agreement, had been concluded. There – I quote from the "Tyrol daily" – Mr. Ex-Ambassador Ludwig Steiner as one who was there even at the time said, among other things, that it was a difficult path, but that we can be proud of the achieved one. I totally agree. He also said it was a very emotional concern. For many years there have been many difficulties. Austria then succeeded – this made sense – in bringing the whole thing to the UN and anchoring the South Tyrol question internationally. That was right and good. Also the autonomy that is now available is one that is actually a very special and very good case of autonomy, which is also often described internationally as a positive example of what an autonomy regulation can look like. Ambassador Steiner said in South Tyrol, in Bolzano, just over a month ago: There is no need for a treaty within the EU to live in peace with the neighbouring country. . That is one of the reasons why we will not agree to this motion for a resolution with regard to any constitutional reform that may happen once; for the current one planned during this legislative period has today been carried to the grave, as Eva Glavischnig said, “unpumpous”. That's one of the reasons: In a united Europe, we do not believe that the anchoring of protection functions for ethnic groups in other countries is necessary. In addition, minority and ethnic groups policy is a matter of active politics and not of confessions which in reality have no significance at all. I am very happy about it and I also welcome Austria's very strong support for South Tyrol and South Tyrol. I have experienced this myself when I studied in Innsbruck in the 1970s and early 1980s, and have had to do with many South Tyroleans who were able to study with Austrians because of their equality, and this is still possible, even in spite of the changed conditions for the universities. That's something positive and meaningful. But the question is, why today, shortly before the election, this request is on the agenda. As I have already said, the role of protection in the European Union is, in our opinion, obsolete. [[Abg. Scheibner: This is what we have decided unanimously in the President-in-Office!]] To give an example of what this autonomy means, this very good autonomy for South Tyrol: South Tyrol is much better in financial terms than any Austrian state. For example, when I compare South Tyrol and North Tyrol, South Tyrol has a third less population, but it has a double budget, which is quite the same for South Tyrol, because simply 80 percent of taxes and taxes remain in South Tyrol. It is good for the country that it can develop well; no one wants to change it. I trust that this good relationship, which has also been achieved through the efforts of Austria over the last decades, can continue. But one thing is interesting: Problems in the relationship between Austria and Italy South Tyrol only exists if in Italy a right government is in power – like the last, Berlusconi, who wanted German Chancellor Schüssel very much success for the elections; fortunately he did not have that, Mr Berlusconi and his Adlates. [[Abg. Neudeck: It was the other way round! Not Berlusconi has wished you success...!]] There are problems only with right-wing governments in Italy – for example, when the Lega Nord wanted to make difficulties – not with governments such as the current one under Prime Minister Prodi, whose alliance was already supported by the South Tyrol People's Party, because it knows that a government of Prodi, a government that is not right, very probably takes into account the interests of ethnic groups, of minorities – which the German-speaking South Tyroleans in South Tyrol are not – I know that a left government, if They want to be very supportive of such groups. The problem is, as I have said, only if rights that are close to the EIA are in the government, and not otherwise. For the Greens, it is completely undisputed that we want the protection of ethnic groups; I think that is clear to everyone here. We are those who think that the protection of minorities, of ethnic groups, is one of the highest goods in a democracy, for living together in a democracy. This includes language, the promotion of bilingualism and multilingualism in schools, but also on town boards. This great asset of protection of ethnic groups, of minorities, applies to South Tyrol, and even when I was a youth there, I have seen admirably trilingual town boards, and I have already wondered in Carinthia that there was almost one language there. However, there is a great deal of good for Carinthia, and I see that, Mr President of the National Council Khol, who you want to talk about, is quite a deficit that your government, which you belong to as an ÖVP member, has not managed to set up bilingual local boards in southern Carinthia. That's something I can only say about: The protection of ethnic groups does not take this federal government seriously! Otherwise, she would have done this for a long time and would not have always apologised for just one country captain, Haider, but did nothing to make sure that he finally put up the long-required bilingual town boards there. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] One word for the BZÖ: I think it's amazing that you're now with Mr. Veit Sounde has a candidate on your list, who not only found the NS employment policy quite impressive – he also said – but also said – and he himself has Slovenian roots, and therefore it is even more incomprehensible to me – that the Slovenians in Carinthia are only guests! [[Abg. Neudeck: Economic policy!]] [[Abg. Rossmann: I too!]] Do you know what Mr Khol would have said if anyone had said that the South Tyrolean Germans were only guests in South Tyrol? - Well, there would have been an uprising! But it is quite right for Carinthia to say that there is no criticism there – not even of you, Mr. National Council President Khol! [[Congratulation with the greens.]] Why now? Why not 1992 when the dispute was settled? This would have been a way of bringing this into a constitutional reform. Why not when Austria joined the EU? There would have been the possibility of adding that as a constitutional change. You didn't do anything. So why now? [[Abg. Scheibner: It's never too late!]] Because there was a petition of the shooters and numerous mayors assigned to the Committee on Petitions? Mr President, you said in an interview that it would be necessary to comply with the Rules of Procedure of the National Council that there is now a resolution on this. – There are many petitions in this House that do not even see the light of the National Council, of this plenary session, but simply treat it in the Committee on Petitions, and are then treated under a day-to-day regulation. Abuses You do not have the Rules of Procedure and Rules of Procedure for something that is your own, very personal interest. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] That is why I can only say: It is a transparent election maneuver that is to be put on the agenda right now. Why not in July? That would have been possible. Just want it now. You make it clear: They protect South Tyrol. As has already been said: We're behind autonomy. That is sensible and necessary. However, this protection function is simply not in the context of the European Union – that is at least what we find – in a contemporary way. To the other protection function that the SPÖ has claimed, i.e. to the protection function of other countries for its groups of people living in Austria: We have a problem with such formulations in principle. For example, I did not know that the Austrian Croats – my colleague Stoisits will report later anyway – would feel that they needed Croatia as a protective force. They demand their rights from the Austrian State, and rightly so. Or the Carinthian Slovenians: I mean, Austria is now being called upon to finally fulfil these rights. It's not like the Carinthian Slovenians had to go to Ljubljana and say: Please, do something! Mr. Khol! Recently, in 2005, you have said to the public that you see it so that Slovenia is not a legal successor to Yugoslavia and therefore has no right to such a protection function for the Carinthian Slovenians. – Now you have at once changed your opinion and agreed to this request. That's strange already, and that's happening shortly before the election. To conclude: A protection function as you now want to anchor them in a constitutional reform that is not taking place at the moment has zero real relevance in a united Europe. That is why we are voting against it, because we are not in favour of a cheap instrumentation of the protection of groups. It must not be a confession policy, but actual actions to enforce the rights of the ethnic groups and minorities, in South Tyrol and in Carinthia.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-09-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00163_d8e9788	The next item is Mr Dr. Khol. Please, Mr President.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-09-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00163_d8e9825	Ladies and gentlemen! I am very pleased that the Foreign Policy Committee has dealt with the petition of the South Tyrolean Sagittarius, the Northern Tyrolian Sagittarius and the East-Tirolian Sagittarius, supported by 113 of the 116 mayors of South Tyrol, and today recommends that the House take into account the concerns of this petition and anchor the protection function of Austria for its South Tyrol countrymen in a constitutional reform in this Constitution. I think that's important. I think that's right. And, Mrs Lunacek, it would have been an increase of a hundred thousand signatures if we had not treated this petition today, but had thrown it into the trash bin. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and the Freeman – BZÖ.]] I'm just saying something about it: Every step the shooters have taken in exercising their right of petition guaranteed in our constitution has been agitated in Rome. There has been unrest in the decision-making process in the subcommittee, and today's debate is being followed with great interest. To be able to deal with these things in full sovereignty, would we have to go all the way again? I think it's right. The shooters have submitted the petition. All three parties who are behind this report today have already addressed this question in the Austrian Convention. I am glad that Mr Niederwieser, Mr Wittauer and Mr Spindelegger have tabled a motion for a resolution and I would ask you to accept this motion for a resolution. I also ask the Greens, Mrs. Lunacek, to jump over her shadow. The concern of South Tyrol is far too important to pull it down into party policy arguments. One thing I would like to say to you: The argument you have put forward that this would not be necessary in Europe is a very dangerous argument. The Italian Government has taken note of Austria's protection function, never leaving a doubt that the Gruber-De Gasperi Agreement, which respects the declaration of dispute and also the general international law of the government, is being observed by the government as it is currently ruling in Italy. And there have been several situations in recent years – the Italian constitutional reform, for example – in which there have been attacks on autonomy when one wanted to establish a general orientation and coordination authority for the headquarters. We intervened, the protection function has taken hold, and Italy has always responded. So that means: The protection function has by no means become obsolete through our accession to the European Union. This is the argument of quite a number of legal groups in Italy – not the majority – and especially Franco Frattini, then Minister of Justice, now EU Commissioner, who seems to have a personal South Tyrol Trauma, who repeatedly points out: In the united Europe, Gruber-De Gasperi is anachronistic. They say the same thing. I'll tell you: The protection function of Austria for South Tyrol is no more and no less than the Gruber-De Gasperi Agreement, General International Law and Dispute Resolution in 1992. All of this is the Austrian Protection function! [[Congratulations with the ÖVP, the Free – BZÖ and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] Today's decision does not mean that Jota will or will not get away from it for the protection function. It does not change international law! But I should like to say that, Mrs Lunacek, I have already done so within the state. First: I firmly believe that the positive forces in this House can and will make important progress in the constitutional reform next year on the basis of the report of the Austrian Convention, which we have discussed today. This is a major project, as the Federal Chancellor Schüssel also explained. And if we change the Constitution, then we will take into account the foreign policy issue of the vast majority of the Austrians and of course allow South Tyrol, our Austrian people's group in South Tyrol, suitable space in this Constitution. And we will not only put in place this symbolic act, but thus make the exercise of the protection function a constitutional obligation of any government in Austria. There is then a constitutional obligation – and no longer a discretion of foreign policy. That's the big difference! And this difference is important for the South Tyroleans, and this difference is also important to me. Ladies and gentlemen! Europe has left the South Tyrol autonomy completely untouched in its legal system. The ethnic proportion continues to exist and the protection function of Austria continues to exist. But Europe has also created something great for South Tyrol: We have produced the national unit in the united Europe. North Tyrol, South Tyrol, East Tyrol: They can all work together economically, culturally, socially. The Brenner border has become invisible, it only exists more in the minds of some. We've become a Tyrol again. That's all we need. There are no border changes in the united Europe, and all of this is not necessary in the united Europe. If, however, anyone lays their hands on autonomy, Austria is ready, then Austria will exercise its international rights, and we in Parliament and also the citizens of this country can remind each Austrian government of their constitutional obligations. That is why today a thanks to the shooters, a thanks to the mayors who did not support this less risk under incitement, for the prefect has opened a criminal proceedings against all the mayors. There's been hearings. They risked it, the procedures were fortunately discontinued. I say again: Austria and Italy are friendly countries. We don't have any open problems. It is our sovereignty to take into account the heart of South Tyrol in our constitution. I ask you again: Vote with everyone! It is an important sign to our compatriots of Ladin and Austrian tongues on the other side of the burner. Live the country of Tyrol!

ParlaMint-AT_2006-09-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00163_d8e9938	Madam President. Federal Minister! High house! In the result and from the intentions, I share the words of my predecessor Andreas Khol. In one point or another, there is a differentiated position in the approach. But what is important is what is contained in this resolution and what will then be decided. The topic of South Tyrol has been much more concerned with emotions – also South Tyrol discussions here in the Hohe Haus – than is the case today. South Tyrol has become part of normality. There are no huge problems, there is nothing more comparable to what in 1962 led Bruno Kreisky to introduce this topic to the UN. Historically, South Tyrol has been part of Austria for many centuries. In 1946, the Gruber-De Gaspari Agreement created at least the basis for autonomy. 1992 – the dispute resolution here in the Hohen Haus. Perhaps you remember – Mr Schieder also spoke about this at the time – that there were already different positions at the time. The speakers here have all said: Of course, Austria continues to feel obliged to ensure that this autonomy is preserved, that it may be increased in the substance, but not reduced. The official Italian position was already: The dispute is settled, and there is no longer this function. That was formally explained to us at the time. In practice all these years almost every Austrian federal government has discussed problems with the Italian government in consultation with the Austrian minority in South Tyrol, the Subcommittee on Bolzano and South Tyrol has visited and talked to all parties, all the political groups there. In practice, this function of Austria has never been doubted. [[Abg. Dr. Khol: That's right!]] She's been recognized, she hasn't been formal. That's reality. It essentially does not change anything by this today's decision. The Austrian position, as it has always been represented, is confirmed only once again today. However, one of the changes in this decision – and I would like to point out that this is indeed something that has not yet been done, at least as a declaration of will by Parliament – is something which Mr Lunacek does not seem to need for me to be inventive, namely that Austria or that this House says by a majority: We not only use something for ourselves, a protection function, but also we have minorities and respect that the countries whose minorities can also exercise such a function! You're right, colleague Lunacek: Why didn't you do that earlier? You could have done that earlier. The wish was expressed very early, in 1992, when EU accession, precisely on this data. Well, now the Convention has come to an end, now it is actually about a new constitution. For us, the position of the minority has always been the absolute priority, both in Slovenia and in what is going on in South Tyrol. That is, as social democrats and social democrats, we do not go so far as to say: We are smarter and better know what is good for you and you want to! as you do now, but instead we say: Okay, this wish is deposited! Whether you really need it or not, you can certainly argue about it, and also about what changes in the substance! But for us this is something we respect! This is not an invention of Andreas Khol and of me, but it is a wish that has often been made to us and which we respect and for which there is now an opportunity to include it in the Austrian Constitution. I have always meant that this would have been the position of the Greens, namely, to focus on what the minority itself wants, and not on the minority, in the sense of what is better for them. This does not seem to be what I also regret in this case. What I consider important in this context is the indication that it is essential for us to have the interests of the minority in South Tyrol in the area of Austrian minority. That is why I am saying this because there is now another choice ahead and because we are still in agreement with God in this discussion in the matter itself. In this context, I address very specific Chancellor Dr. Schüssel and the third Chancellor. April 2006, when he really caused white horror in South Tyrol. In this situation, Federal Chancellor Schüssel flies to Rome, embraces Silvio Berlusconi, his dear friend and wishes him a brilliant election victory. Blank horror in South Tyrol. This is one of the important benefits of the European Union. In spite of all this, it should not be forgotten that, despite the fact that Austria and Italy are democratic states or have acted after the Second World War, the contrasts were so great that it took so many decades for one to find this compensation to experience a really functioning autonomy, so that the opposites between the groups have practically disappeared, except for a few who are still trying to create opponents. At the beginning, however, there was an act against international law, namely that a part of Austria was separated against all the principles. After the First World War, the peoples' right of self-simulation was one of the principles they tried to implement in Europe. – In South Tyrol, on the other hand, people were not asked how they saw themselves and where they had seen themselves. I think that is already a special feature that South Tyrol highlights somewhat from other regions in Europe where there are minorities of other ethnic groups. That is why we have to signal – and I believe that this is also what the people of South Tyrol call for – even if there are no current problems that we want to point out this particular protection function in our federal constitution. Mrs Lunacek, you have opposed yourself: On the one hand, you have said that there is no need, because there are no problems either, and there is no need for confession in a constitution, but in the next sentence, there are problems only if there are legal governments at work in Italy. . I tell you, I do not care which government in Italy causes problems for our Austrian population in South Tyrol. [[Congratulations with the liberals – BZÖ and the ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen, we have discussed these issues very intensively in the Constitutional Convention. I am all the more sorry that we have not reached a consensus in the Convention as a whole and that there is no new Constitution, because then we should not have decided on a motion for a resolution today. Mrs Lunacek, you are asking: Why right now? – We unanimously adopted this agenda in the President – as your representatives have also been in it – which is already stated in July. The election already plays a role, but not in the sense that we want to score with it, but because we have said we want to have a theme on which there is a broad consensus that is held out of the normal election debate. It is a pity that today we will only discuss and vote on a resolution. I would rather have decided this already in a constitutional novel and also decided it. In the Convention, we made the proposal to extend the commitment to protection, including those old-Austrian minorities who still live in other European countries, such as the successor states of the Austro-Hungarian monarchy. In recent decades, these have also suffered very, very unpleasant, expulsion and oppression. Only since the turn of these former Communist states has there been a chance for those who remain to use their own customs, their own language and thus to have a future as a people group. There's something Austria has failed to do. I remember that after the turn of the Czech Republic, we had requests from German-speaking schools to support them, so that at least there are schoolbooks; also from other areas, such as associations who have said: We see ourselves as descendants of Austria, the Austrian People's Group – and not the German! At the beginning of the nineties, Austria said: We don't care, that's none of our business; go to Germany! That is why there are Goethe-Institute in these countries, which is why there are subsidies from Germany, but, unfortunately, very little has been made from Austria. This should also be reflected in the fact that we also have a historical basis where there are small remains of old-Austrian minorities – in the Czech Republic, in Croatia, in Slovakia, in Hungary and in Slovenia – where there is a representation and protection function. I hope that in the next negotiations on a Constitutional novel, we will be able not only to implement this protection function against South Tyrol, but also towards the old-Austrian minorities in other European countries.

Together with the Paris Treaty, the basis for South Tyrol autonomy was created. Although this Treaty has not brought South Tyroleans the right of self-determination required, it requires the State of Italy to grant South Tyrol autonomy. This anniversary is an occasion for memory. Ladies and gentlemen of the Greens: For this very reason, I do not understand them, because it is incomprehensible that you, in particular, reject this protection for an Austrian minority abroad. In my native Tyrol, there is a long-growing cross-border cooperation in the cultural sector, in the economic field, in the care of tradition, and even great commitment. I am therefore particularly pleased that, with the constitutional anchoring of the protective role, we are fulfilling a long-standing wish of our South Tyrol friends, a wish expressed by our South Tyrolean friends at the EU accession of Austria. I thank all the Members who have dealt with the petition of the shooters and mayors. As a result, I would like to thank all those who will decide on this proposal by a large majority today.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-09-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00163_d8e11437	Mr President. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Values Representatives of NGOs! It is indeed, I think, often so that one cannot imagine what it means to live with a disability in a country in which there is hardly any social security in which not all people have access to health care – which is, in most cases, still the case with us and hopefully will remain the same – but in which all this is not available. We cannot imagine what happens if there is no opportunity in labour law to actually get care, if you have had an accident at work and thus have a disability at life, for example, if there is no accident right or no accident insurance that comes in when someone suffers an accident and then has a permanent or temporary disability and ensure that there is the right to go back, for example, after an occupation. All of this is a matter of course for most people. - I still assume that. I know it's not quite that way, but almost. In developing countries, in countries that hardly have such a security, as has been said, it can often be very difficult for people to live their lives, to pursue a profession, to keep a job, and, if necessary, to provide for the family even if a disability occurs. I think it is necessary to stress, even in development cooperation, the importance of this, and to make it clear that this is a right that people with disabilities have, and not alms that are then “the poor” – under quotations. It is about the right of people with disabilities to live as well in every society as possible with the existing technical possibilities. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP as well as on representatives of the Freeman – BZÖ.]] Second point: Legal form neutral corporate taxation. We no longer want to: here individual companies and individual companies, there capital companies, GmbHs, stock companies, but we want an attractive, flat corporate tax, 25 percent or less for all accounting companies. We want to take the next big step for the economic and work location. Third key pillar of the tax reform, which is planned: Abolition of charges. We believe that the best, even towards simplification, is the complete abolition of taxes. First point: We want to abolish the advertising charge. Second point: We want to abolish the corporate tax. Third point: Legal Business Fees – think of credit fees, think of leasing contracts, stock contracts! – we want to abolish it because it makes no sense for the location Austria. Fourth point: The abolition of inheritance and gift tax has already been addressed. That makes it clear: This federal government is facing the population and says: We want to continue a way of reducing taxes! We stand for discharge, we want to give people more freedom, we want performance to be better rewarded in our country! ParlaMint-AT_2006-09-21-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00163_d8e18066	Mr President. Federal Minister! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Mrs Bayr, you are expected to have gone a long way in the last few years, because the Kyoto target is an ambitious target of the Federal Government. If you add a little bit of what the Federal Government has done in recent years in this direction – for example, I am thinking only of the particle filters, of the thermal old house renovation, the many bio-plants, the district heating, then now for the future the 500-million-€-energy foundation et cetera – you will see: Much has happened and much will still be done. [[Abg. Bayr: We are further away from the finish line!]] But back to the Alpine Convention: This is an international agreement for the protection of the natural area and for the promotion of sustainable development in the Alps. The Convention also places great emphasis on safeguarding the economic and cultural interests of the local population in the signatory states. The ever-increasing demand by humans is increasingly threatening the Alpine region and its ecological functions. The resulting damage can usually not be remedied or only with high effort, with considerable costs and usually only over long periods of time. This leads to the need to bring economic interests into line with environmental requirements, as our principle of the eco-social market economy provides. For Austria, the Alpine region is of significant importance as a geographical unit. With more than 28 percent of the total application area, the Austrian Alpine share is the largest in the Alpine Convention, and it covers almost 65 percent of our state territory. 40 percent of the total population Austria lives in the Alps, divided into 1,135 municipalities – out of a total of 5,934 municipalities throughout the Convention area. Austria is therefore the state in which the Alps take the largest proportion of land and population in the state area. The traffic protocol is often referred to as the heart of the Alpine Convention. It commits to a coordinated environmental and transport policy which limits transport-related loads and risks and thus takes into account the interests of the environment, society and the economy. The Parties agreed to abandon the construction of new high-level roads for the cross-border traffic of the Alps. In terms of cost truth, the undersigned have agreed on the 'polluter pays' principle. Austria has ratified the traffic protocol as one of the first countries, and since 18. December 2005 shall constitute the implementing protocols in force. Under the chairmanship of Austria at the Alpine Convention, a multi-annual plan has been completed, and for this our Minister for the Environment, our Minister for the Environment, Josef Pröll, deserves a warm thanks. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] [[Abg. Krainer: Yes, please lie flat! Or is it enough to kneel?]] This multi-annual work programme provides the necessary framework and support to not only maintain a fragile ecosystem across the area, but also develop it further, as the Alpine Convention provides in its holistic impetus. It is now up to Italy to ratify the Transport Protocol and open the way for an EU-wide ratification to ensure that the same minimum standards apply throughout the Alps. - Thank you.
